=== PDF PAGE 676 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 656 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
Start Operation
Set TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously. 
TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm is a trigger bit, 
which is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm (master and slave 
channels) is set to 1 and the counters of master 
channel and slave channels 1 and 2 start.
INTTAUAnIm is generated on the master channel 
and TAUAnTTOUTm (slave channel 1) is set.
During Operation
TAUAnCDRm can be changed at any time.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnRSF.RSFm can be 
read at any time.
TAUAnRDT.TAUAnRDTm can be changed 
during operation.
TAUAnCNTm of master channel and slave 
channels 1 and 2 load TAUAnCDRm value and 
count down. 
When the counter of master channel reaches 
0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm (master) is generated.
•
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into 
TAUAnCNTm (master) to continue count 
operation.
•
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into 
TAUAnCNTm (slave 1/2) to start countdown.
•
TAUAnTTOUTm (slave 1) is set.
When TAUAnCNTm (slave 1) reaches 0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm (slave 1) is generated.
•
TAUAnTTOUTm (slave 1) is reset.
When TAUAnCNTm (slave 2) reaches 0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm (slave 2) is generated.
•
TAUAnTTOUTm (slave 3) is set.
•
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into 
TAUAnCNTm (slave 3) to start a countdown 
operation.
When TAUAnCNTm (slave 3) reaches 0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm (slave 3) is generated.
•
TAUAnTTOUTm (slave 3) is reset.
Stop Operation
Set TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously.
TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm is a trigger bit, which is 
automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm is cleared to 0 and the 
counter stops.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnTTOUTm stop and 
retain their current values.

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
13.14.4
One-Shot Pulse Output Function
(1)
Overview
Summary
This function outputs a signal pulse with a specific pulse width and delay time 
(both defined relative to an external input signal pulse) by using a master and a 
slave channel. The delay time is specified using the master channel. The pulse 
width is specified using the slave channel. 
Prerequisites
• Two channels
• The operating mode for the master channel should be set to one-count 
mode. (See table 13-111, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Master Channels of 
One-Shot Pulse Output Function.)
• The operating mode for slave channels should be set to pulse one-count 
mode. (See table 13-114, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave Channels of 
One-Shot Pulse Output Function.)
• TAUAnTTOUTm is not used with the master channel of this function.
• The channel output mode for the slave channel should be set to 
independent channel output mode 2. (See section 13.8, Channel Output 
Modes.)
• TAUAnTTINm (master) has to be detected while TAUAnCNTm (master) and 
TAUAnCNTm (slave) await a trigger. Furthermore, the slave is only 
triggered by an interrupt from the master channel and not by TAUAnTTINm 
(slave).
Functional
description
The counters are enabled by setting the channel trigger bits 
(TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm) to 1. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm, enabling count 
operation. 
• Master channel:
When the next valid TAUAnTTINm input edge is detected, the current value 
of TAUAnCDRm is loaded into TAUAnCNTm. The counter starts to count 
down from this value. If TAUAnCMORm.MD0 = 0, a trigger (TAUAnTTINm) 
which is detected within the delay time is ignored.
When the counter of master channel reaches 0000H, INTTAUAnIm is 
generated. The counter is reset to FFFFH and waits for the next valid 
TAUAnTTINm input edge.
• Slave channels:
INTTAUAnIm generated on master channel triggers the counter operation of 
slave channel. The current value of TAUAnCDRm (slave) is loaded into 
TAUAnCNTm (slave). The counter starts counting down from this value. An 
interrupt occurs and the TAUAnTTOUTm signal is set.
When the counter reaches 0001H, INTTAUAnIm is generated and 
TAUAnTTOUTm signal is reset. The counter stops at 0000H and waits for 
the next INTTAUAnIm of master channel.
The counter can be stopped by setting TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and 
slave channels to 1. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm to 0. TAUAnCNTm and 
TAUAnTTOUTm of master and slave channels stop but their values are 
retained. The counter can be restarted by setting TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm to 1. 
Setting TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm to 1 while counting allows the counter to restart 
counting of master channel without making a stop (forced restart).


=== PDF PAGE 680 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 660 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
Conditions
• If TAUAnCMORn.TAUAnMD0 of master channel is set to 0, TAUAnTTINm 
input edges detected during counting are ignored.
• Simultaneous rewrite can be used with this function. See section 13.7, 
Simultaneous Rewrite.
Equations
Delay to input pulse = (TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1)  count clock cycle
Pulse width = (TAUAnCDRm (slave))  count clock cycle

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
13.14.5
Offset Trigger Output Function
(1)
Overview
Summary
This function generates a PWM output using a master channel and a slave 
channel, enabling the pulse width (duration) of the TAUAnTTOUTm to be set. 
The pulse cycle is set by detecting a valid input edge of master channel. The 
pulse width is specified on the slave channel. 
Prerequisites
• Two channels
• The operating mode for the master channel should be set to capture mode. 
(See table 13-119, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Master Channels of Offset 
Trigger Output Function.)
• The operating mode for slave channels should be set to one-count mode. 
(See table 13-122, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave Channels of Offset 
Trigger Output Function.)
• The output mode for slave channels should be set to independent channel 
output mode 2. (See section 13.8, Channel Output Modes.)
• TAUAnTTOUTm is not used with the master channel of this function.
Functional
description
The counter can be started by setting the channel trigger bit 
(TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm) to 1. This makes TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 1, enabling 
the counter to count up. The master channel counter (TAUAnCNTm) starts to 
count up from 0000H. 
• Master channels:
When a valid TAUAnTTINm input edge is detected, the current value of the 
counter (TAUAnCNTm) is loaded into the data register of master channel 
(TAUAnCDRm). INTTAUAnIm is generated and the counter restarts to count 
up from 0000H.
• Slave channels:
The INTTAUAnIm of master channel sets the TAUAnTTOUTm (slave) signal 
and triggers the counter of the slave channel. The current value of 
TAUAnCDRm (slave) is loaded into TAUAnCNTm (slave) and the counter 
starts to count down from this value. 
When the counter reaches 0000H (duty time has elapsed), INTTAUAnIm is 
generated and TAUAnTTOUTm signal is reset. The counter returns to 
FFFFH and awaits the next INTTAUAnIm of master channel.
The counter can be stopped by setting TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm to 1 for master 
and slave channels. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm to 0. TAUAnCNTm and 
TAUAnTTOUTm of master and slave channels stop but retain their values. The 
counters can be restarted by setting TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm to 1.


=== PDF PAGE 689 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 669 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
(2)
Equations
Pulse width = (TAUAnCDRm (slave) + 1)  count clock cycle
Duty cycle [%] = [TAUAnCDRm (slave)/TAUAnTTINm cycle + 1)]  100
– Duty cycle = 0% 
TAUAnCDRm (slave) = 0000H
– Duty cycle = 100% 
TAUAnCDRm (slave)  TAUAnTTINm cycle + 1

10: Detection of falling and rising edges
11: Disables setting.

V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
13.14.6
Triangle PWM Output Function
(1)
Overview
Summary
This function generates multiple triangle PWM outputs by using a master and
one or more slave channels. It enables the pulse cycle (frequency) and the
duty cycle of TAUAnTTOUTm to be set using the master and slave channels 
respectively. 
The master channel generates a carrier cycle. The first cycle of master 
channel controls the down status and the second cycle controls the up status 
of the slave counter.
Prerequisites
• Two channels
• The operating mode for master channels should be set to interval timer 
mode. (See table 13-127, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Master Channels of 
Triangle PWM Output Function.)
• The operating mode for slave channels should be set up/down count mode. 
(See table 13-131, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave Channels of Triangle 
PWM Output Function.)
• The channel output mode for master channels should be set to independent 
channel output mode 1. (See section 13.8, Channel Output Modes.)
• The channel output mode for slave channels should be set to synchronous 
channel output mode 2. (See section 13.8, Channel Output Modes.)
• The following settings allows TAUAnTTOUTm to be at high level during the 
down status of a carrier cycle.
– If TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 (master) bit is set to 0, 
TAUAnTO.TAUAnTOm should be set to 1 while 
TAUAnTOE.TAUAnTOEm is set to 0 (recommended setting).
– If TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 (master) bit is set to 1, 
TAUAnTO.TAUAnTOm should be set to 0 while 
TAUAnTOE.TAUAnTOEm is set to 0 (recommended setting).
Functional
description
The counters are started by setting the channel trigger bit 
(TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm) to 1 for every channel. This in turn sets 
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm, enabling count operation.
The current values of TAUAnCDRm (master and slave) are loaded into
TAUAnCNTm (master and slave) and the counters start counting down from
these values. When the TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 bit of master channel is 
set to 1, an interrupt is generated and TAUAnTTOUTm signal of master 
toggles.
• Master channels:
When the counter of master channel reaches 0000H (pulse cycle time
has elapsed), INTTAUAnIm is generated and the TAUAnTTOUTm signal
toggles. TAUAnCNTm then reloads the TAUAnCDRm value and counts
down.


=== PDF PAGE 697 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 677 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
• Slave channels:
The INTTAUAnIm of master channel triggers the counter of the slave
channel:
– If the slave counter is counting down, the count direction changes.
– If the slave counter is counting up, TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded and 
the counter starts to count down.
When the counter of the slave channel reaches 0001H while counting up or 
down, INTTAUAnIm is generated and the TAUAnTTOUTm (slave) signal is 
set/reset.
The counter continues count-up/-down and waits for the next INTTAUAnlm 
of master channel. 
Setting TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm allows TAUAnTTOUTm signal switching 
between normal phase and reverse phase during operation. 
The counter can be stopped by setting TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and 
slave channels to 1. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 0. TAUAnCNTm and 
TAUAnTTOUTm of master and slave channels stop but retain their values.
Conditions
This function enables simultaneous rewrite. See section 13.7, Simultaneous 
Rewrite.
(2)
Equations
Pulse cycle = (TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1)  count clock cycle
0000H < TAUAnCDRm (master) < FFFFH
Carrier cycle (down/up) = (TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1)  2  count clock cycle
Duty cycle [%] = 
[(TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1 - TAUAnCDRm (slave))/(TAUAnCDRm (master) + 
1)]  100
– Duty cycle = 100 % 
TAUAnCDRm (slave) = 0000H
– Duty cycle = 0 % 
TAUAnCDRm (slave)  TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1

TAUAnCDRm (Slave) = 0000H
• If TAUAnCDRm (slave) = 0000H, INTTAUAnlm is not generated while the 
counter of slave channel is counting up. TAUAnTTOUTm remains at the 
active level because there is no reset signal of TAUAnTTOUTm to be 
detected.

V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
13.14.7
Triangle PWM Output Function with Dead Time
(1)
Overview
Summary
This function generates multiple triangle PWM outputs with a predefined dead 
time added by using a master and two or more slave channels. The resulting 
PWM signals are output via TAUAnTTOUTm of the slave channel 3, enabling 
the pulse cycle (frequency) and the duty cycle of TAUAnTTOUTm to be set 
using the master and slave channels.
Carrier cycles are generated on master channel. The first pulse controls the 
down status of slave counter and the second one controls the up status. 
An interrupt on slave 2 causes TAUAnTTOUTm of slave channels to be set/
reset. Depending on the settings of TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm, delay time is 
added to positive or negative logic side of the signal (i.e., whether 
TAUAnTTOUTm is set/reset immediately or after dead time has elapsed). The 
duration of the dead time is specified by slave channel 3.
Prerequisites
• Three channels. For slave channels 2 and 3, select even channel CH (a) 
and odd channel CH (a + 1).
• The operating mode for master channels should be set to interval timer 
mode. (See table 13-137, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Master Channels of 
Triangle PWM Output Function with Dead Time.)
• The operating mode for slave channel 2 should be set to up/down count 
mode. (See table 13-141, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave Channel 2 of 
Triangle PWM Output Function with Dead Time.) Slave channel 2 should be 
an even channel.
• The operating mode for slave channel 3 should be set to one-count mode. 
(See table 13-145, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave Channel 3 of Triangle 
PWM Output Function with Dead Time.) Slave channel 3 should be an odd 
channel.
• The channel output mode for master channels should be set to independent 
channel output mode 1. (See section 13.8, Channel Output Modes.)
• The output mode for slave channels 2 and 3 should be set to synchronous 
channel output mode 2 with dead time output. (See section 13.8, Channel 
Output Modes.)
• The following settings make a TAUAnTTOUTm signal at high level during 
the down status of the carrier cycle:
– If TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 (master) bit is set to 0, 
TAUAnTO.TAUAnTOm should be set to 1 while 
TAUAnTOE.TAUAnTOEm is set to 0. (recommended setting)
– If TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 (master) bit is set to 1, 
TAUAnTO.TAUAnTOm should be set to 0 while 
TAUAnTOE.TAUAnTOEm is set to 0. 
Note
The triangle PWM output function with dead time does not use slave channel 
1.


=== PDF PAGE 708 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 688 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
Functional
description
The counter starts by setting the channel trigger bit (TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm) to 
1. This makes TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 1, enabling count operation. The 
current value of TAUAnCDRm is loaded into TAUAnCNTm and the counter 
starts to count down from the TAUAnCDRm value. If 
TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 bit of master channel is set to 1, an interrupt is 
generated and the master's TAUAnTTOUTm signal is toggled.
• Master channels:
When the counter of master channel reaches 0000H, an INTTAUAnIm is 
generated and the TAUAnTTOUTm signal is toggled. The TAUAnCDRm 
value is reloaded to continue countdown.
• Slave channel 2:
If INTTAUAnIm is generated on the master channel, the counter of slave 
channel 2 is triggered.
– If the slave counter is counting down, the counting direction changes.
– If the slave counter is counting up, the TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded 
and the counter starts to count down.
The counter continues to count down/up and waits for the next INTTAUAnlm 
of master channel. 
• Slave channel 3:
If INTTAUAnIm is generated on slave channel 2, the counter of slave 
channel 3 is triggered. The current value of TAUAnCDRm (slave 3) is 
loaded into TAUAnCNTm (slave 3) and the counter starts to count down 
from the TAUAnCDRm value. 
When the counter reaches 0000H, INTTAUAnIm occurs. The counter 
returns to FFFFH and waits for the next INTTAUAnlm of slave channel 2. 
As described in table 13-136, Operation of TAUAnTTOUTm upon Occurrence 
of an Interrupt on Slave Channel 2, the set/reset timing (right after occurrence 
of an interrupt or after dead time has elapsed) depends on the 
TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm setting of the corresponding channel.
The setting of TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm also determines whether a high level 
signal (TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm = 0) or a low level signal 
(TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm = 1) is output from the corresponding channel. 
The counter can be stopped by setting TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and 
slave channels to 1. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm to 0. TAUAnCNTm and 
TAUAnTTOUTm of master and slave channels stop but retain their values. 
TAUAnTTOUTm can be 100 % output by setting the TAUAnCDRm value of 
slave channel 2 to 0000H.
Conditions
This function enables simultaneous rewrite. See section 13.7, Simultaneous 
Rewrite.
TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm and TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm should be set before 
start of count operation. Slave channels 2 and 3 should have the opposite 
settings of TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm or TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm.

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
(2)
Equations
Pulse cycle = (TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1)  count clock cycle
0000H  TAUAnCDRm (master) < FFFFH
Carrier cycle (down/up) = (TAUAnCDRm (master) +1)  2  count clock cycle
PWM signal width (normal phase) = [(TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1 - 
TAUAnCDRm (slave 2))  2 - (TAUAnCDRm (slave 3) + 1)]  count clock cycle
PWM signal width (reverse phase) = [(TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1 -
TAUAnCDRm (slave 2))  2 + (TAUAnCDRm (slave 3) + 1)]  count clock cycle

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
The following settings apply to the general timing diagram.
• Master channels:
– INTTAUAnIm is generated at the beginning of operation. 
(TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 = 1)
• Slave channel 2:
– INTTAUAnIm is not generated at the beginning of operation. 
(TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 = 0)
– TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm = 0
– Positive logic (TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm = 0)
• Slave channel 3:
– INTTAUAnIm is generated at the beginning of operation. 
(TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 = 1)
– TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm = 1
– Negative logic (TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm = 1)

(7)
Operating Procedure for Triangle PWM Output Function with Dead Time
Table 13-149
Operating Procedure for Triangle PWM Output Function with Dead Time
Operation
TAUAn Status
Initial Channel Setting
Master channels: Set TAUAnCMORm/
TAUAnCMURm register and the channel
output mode as described in (4) Register 
Settings for Master Channels.
Slave channel 2: Set TAUAnCMORm/
TAUAnCMURm register and the channel
output mode as described in (5) Register 
Settings for Slave Channel 2.
Slave channel 3: Set TAUAnCMORm/
TAUAnCMURm register and the channel output 
mode as described in (6) Register Settings for 
Slave Channel 3.
Set the value of TAUAnCDRm register of every 
channel.
Channel operation is stopped.
Start Operation
Set TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously. 
TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm is a trigger bit, which 
is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm (master and slave channels) 
is set to 1 and the counter of master/slave channel 
starts.
INTTAUAnIm (master) is generated on the master 
channel if TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 is set to 1.
During Operation
TAUAnCDRm can be changed at any time.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnRSF.TAUAnRSFm 
can be read at any time.
TAUAnRDT.TAUAnRDTm can be changed 
during operation.
TAUAnCDRm value of master channel and slave 
channel 2 is loaded into TAUAnCNTm to perform 
counting down. When the counter of master 
channel reaches 0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm (master) is generated.
•
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into 
TAUAnCNTm (master) to continue count 
operation.
•
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into 
TAUAnCNTm (slave 2) or counting is started in 
opposite direction. 
When TAUAnCNTm of slave channel 2 reaches 
0001H:
•
INTTAUAnIm (slave 2) is generated.
•
TAUAnCDRm value of slave channel 3 is loaded 
into TAUAnCNTm perform counting down.
When TAUAnCNTm of slave channel 3 reaches 
0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
Stop Operation
Set TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously.
TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm is a trigger bit, which 
is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm is cleared to 0 and the 
counter stops.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnTTOUTm stop and 
retain their current values.
 Restart

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
13.14.9
Non-Complementary Modulation Output Function Type 1
(1)
Overview
Summary
This function outputs a PWM signal, a high-level signal, or a low-level signal 
from TAUAnTTOUTm depending on the values of the real-time output bits 
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) and the modulation output enable bits 
(TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm) of a pair of slave channels. Three pairs of 
channels are typically used.
Prerequisites
• One master channel and seven slave channels
• The operating mode for master channel should be set to interval timer 
mode. (See table 13-151, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Master Channels of 
Non-Complementary Modulation Output Function Type 1.)
• The operating mode for slave channels 1 to 7 should be set to one-count 
mode. (See table 13-154, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave Channel 1 of 
Non-Complementary Modulation Output Function Type 1.)
• TAUAnTTOUTm is not used with the master channel of this function.
• TAUAnTTOUTm of slave channel 1 is not used with this function, but 
TAUAnTRC.TRCm should be set to 1. (See section 13.8, Channel Output 
Modes.)
• The channel output mode for slave channels 2 to 7 should be set to 
synchronous channel output mode 1 with non-complementary modulation 
output. (See section 13.8, Channel Output Modes.)
• TAUAnCDRm of slave channel 1 should be set to 0000H.
Functional
description
The master/slave channel counter is enabled by setting the channel trigger bit 
(TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm) to 1. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 1, enabling 
count operation. The value of data register (TAUAnCDRm) is loaded into the 
counter (TAUAnCNTm) and the counter starts to count down. When the 
counter reaches 0000H, INTTAUAnlm occurs.
• Slave channel 1:
Slave channel 1 is set as a channel that triggers real-time output
(TAUAnTRC.TAUAnTRCm = 1). If an interrupt occurs on slave channel 1 
(TAUAnCDRm is fixed to 0000H), the value of real-time output bit 
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) of the channel that has detected the interrupt 
on slave channel 1 changes. After that, the counter returns to FFFFH and 
waits for the next interrupt of master channel.
• Slave channel 2:
Slave channel 2 generates a PWM output. The master channel specifies a 
PWM output cycle and slave channel 2 specifies a duty cycle. After 
generating an interrupt, the counter returns to FFFFH and awaits the next 
interrupt from the master channel.
Slave channels 3 to 7 operate like slave channel 2.
As described in table 13-150, TAUAnTTOUTm Output from One Pair of Slave 
Channels of Non-Complementary Modulation Output Function Type 1, a signal 
output from TAUAnTTOUTm depends on the value of the real-time output bit 
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) and modulation output bit 
(TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm) of slave channel. 
This function cannot use a forced restart. The counter can be stopped by 
setting TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and slave channels to 1. This sets 
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm to 0. TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnTTOUTm of master and 


=== PDF PAGE 725 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 705 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
slave channels stop but retain their current values. The counter can be 
restarted by setting TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm.
Conditions
• If TAUAnTME.TMEm = 0 on slave channels 2 to 7:
– If the channel's TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm is set to 1, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs a high-level signal.
– If the channel's TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm is set to 0, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs a low-level signal.
• If TAUAnTME.TMEm = 1 on slave channels 2 to 7:
– If the channel's TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm is set to 1, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs PWM corresponding to the channel.
– If the channel's TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm is set to 0, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs a low-level signal.
• If TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm is set to 1, high-level and low-level signals 
output from TAUAnTTOUTm are inverted. There is no impact on PWM 
signal. Only the initial setting of TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm is permitted 
(cannot be changed during operation).
• This function enables simultaneous rewrite. See section 13.7, Simultaneous 
Rewrite.
• TAUAnCDRm value of slave channel 1 should be set to 0000H so that a 
real-time output is triggered at the same time with PWM generation on slave 
channels 2 to 7.
• If TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm is set to 0 on slave channels 2 to 7, 
TAUAnTO.TAUAnTOm is set low before TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm is set to 0.
• If TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm is set to 1 on slave channels 2 to 7, 
TAUAnTO.TAUAnTOm is set high before TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm is set to 0.
(2)
Equations
Slave channels 2 to 7:
PWM output cycle = [TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1]  count clock 
PWM output duty time = [TAUAnCDRm (slave)]  count clock

(d)
Simultaneous Rewrite for Master Channels

settings.

If TAUAnRDS.TAUAnRDSm = 1, it is necessary for an upper channel higher 
than a master channel to generate a simultaneous rewrite trigger signal.

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
Start Operation
Set TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously. (Set only slave 
channels 2 to 7 at a channel restart time.) 
TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm is a trigger bit, which 
is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm of master and slave 
channels is set to 1 and the counter starts counting 
down.
During Operation
TAUAnCDRm, TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm, and 
TAUAnTMEm.TAUAnTMEm can be changed at 
any time.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnRSF.TAUAnRSFm can 
be read at any time.
TAUAnRDT.TAUAnRDTm can be changed 
during operation.
TAUAnCDRm value of master channel, slave 
channel 1 and slave channels 2 to 7 is loaded into 
TAUAnCNTm to perform counting down. When the 
counter of master channel reaches 0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
•
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into 
TAUAnCNTm to continue counting down.
•
PWM output signals of slave channels 2 to 7 
are set/reset.
•
TAUAnCDRm value of slave channel 1 is 
reloaded into TAUAnCNTm to perform counting 
down.
•
TAUAnCDRm value of slave channels 2 to 7 is 
reloaded into TAUAnCNTm to perform counting 
down.
•
When the counter of slave channel 1 or slave 
channels 2 to 7 reaches 0000H:
-
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
•
When the counter of slave channels 2 to 7 
reaches 0000H:
-
PWM output signals of slave channels 2 to 7 
are set/reset.
TAUAnTTOUTm of slave channels 2 to 7 outputs a 
PWM signal, a high-level signal or low-level signal 
depending on the values of real-time output bits 
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) and modulation 
output bit (TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm) of a pair of 
slave channels.
Stop Operation
Set TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously.
TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm is a trigger bit, which 
is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm is cleared to 0 and the 
counter stops.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnTTOUTm stop and 
retain their current values.

V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
13.14.10
Non-Complementary Modulation Output Function Type 2
(1)
Overview
Summary
This function outputs a PWM signal, a high-level signal, or low-level signal 
fromTAUAnTTOUTm depending on the real-time output bit value
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) and the modulation output enable bit value
(TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm) of a pair of slave channels. Three pairs of 
channels are typically used.
Prerequisites
• One master channel and seven slave channels
• The operating mode for the master channel should be set to interval timer 
mode. (See table 13-163, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Master Channels of 
Non-Complementary Modulation Output Function Type 2.)
• The operating mode for slave channel 1 should be set to event count mode. 
(See table 13-167, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave Channel 1 of Non-
Complementary Modulation Output Function Type 2.) 
• The operating mode for slave channels 2 to 7 should be set to up/down 
count mode. (See table 13-170, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave 
Channels 2 to 7 of Non-Complementary Modulation Output Function Type 
2.) 
• This function does not use TAUAnTTOUTm on the master channel.
• This function does not use TAUAnTTOUTm of slave channel 1 but 
TAUAnTRC.TAUAnTRCm should be set to 1. (See section 13.8, Channel 
Output Modes.)
• The channel output mode for slave channels 2 to 7 should be set to 
synchronous channel output mode 2 with non-complementary modulation 
output. (See section 13.8, Channel Output Modes.) 
Functional
description
The counter is enabled by setting the channel trigger bit 
(TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm) to 1. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 1, enabling 
count operation. The value of data register (TAUAnCDRm) is loaded into the 
counter (TAUAnCNTm). 
• Master channels: 
The counter of master channel starts to count down. When the counter 
reaches 0000H, INTTAUAnIm occurs.
• Slave channel 1: 
When slave channel 1 detects an interrupt from the master channel, the 
count decreases by 1. When the counter reaches to 0000H, slave channel 1 
waits from the next interrupt from the master channel. And the 
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into TAUAnCNTm (slave 1) and 
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
Since slave channel 1 is set as a real-time output trigger channel 
(TAUAnTRC.TAUAnTRCm = 1), the real-time output bit 
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) of the channel which detects an interrupt on the 
corresponding channel changes when an interrupt occurs on slave 1 
channel.
• Slave channel 2: 
Once detecting an interrupt from the master channel, TAUAnCNTm counts 
in the reverse direction. When an interrupt is detected during count-up 
operation, TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded and then the counter starts to 
count down.


=== PDF PAGE 738 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 718 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
If TAUAnCNTm = 0001H, an interrupt occurs and a PWM output signal is 
set/reset.
The combined use of the master channel and slave channel 2 generates a 
PWM output signal. The master channel generates a PWM output cycle and 
slave channel 2 generate a duty cycle.
Slave channels 3 to 7 operates like slave channel 2.
A signal that is output from TAUAnTTOUTm depends on a real-time output bit 
value (TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) and a modulation output bit value 
(TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm) of the slave channel, as described in table 13-162, 
TAUAnTTOUTm Output of a Pair of Slave Channels in Non-Complementary 
Modulation Output Function Type 2.
This function cannot make a forced restart. The counter operation can be 
stopped by setting the TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and slave channels to 
1. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm to 0. Both TAUAnCNTm and 
TAUAnTTOUTm of master and slave channels stop but retain their own 
values. The counter can be restarted by setting TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm to 1.
Conditions
• If TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm of slave channel is set to 1:
– If TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of the channel is 1, TAUAnTTOUTm outputs 
the corresponding PWM of the channel.
– If TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of the channel is 0, TAUAnTTOUTm outputs 
a low-level signal.
• If TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm of the slave channel is set to 0:
– If TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of the channel is set to 1, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs a high-level signal.
– If TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of the channel is set to 0, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs a low-level signal.
• If TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm is set to 1, high-level and low-level signals 
output from TAUAnTTOUTm are inverted, having no effect on PWM signals. 
TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm can only be initialized (unchangeable during 
operation).
• Simultaneous rewrite can be used with this function. See section 13.7, 
Simultaneous Rewrite.
• If TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm of slave channels 2 to 7 is set to 0, 
TAUAnTO.TAUAnTOm is set low before setting TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 0.
• If TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm of slave channels 2 to 7 is set to 1, 
TAUAnTO.TAUAnTOm is set high before setting TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 0.

Equations

PWM output duty time
= [TAUAnCDRm (master) + 1 - TAUAnCDRm (slave)]  2  count clock

(c)

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
Start Operation
Set TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously. (Set only slave 
channels 2 to 7 at a channel restart time.) 
TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm is a trigger bit, which 
is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm of master and slave 
channels is set to 1 and the counter starts to count 
down.
During Operation
TAUAnCDRm, TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm, and 
TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm can be changed at 
any time.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnRSF.TAUAnRSFm are 
readable at any time.
TAUAnRDT.TAUAnRDTm is changeable during 
operation.
TAUAnCDRm value of master channel and slave 
channels 2 to 7 is loaded into TAUAnCNTm to 
perform count down. The TAUAnCDRm value of 
slave channel 1 is loaded and the counter waits for 
an interrupt from the master channel. When the 
counter of master channel reaches 0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
•
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into 
TAUAnCNTm to continue counting down.
•
The TAUAnCNTm value of slave channel 1 
decrements by 1 and the counter waits for a 
next interrupt from the master channel.
•
TAUAnCNTm of slave channels 2 to 7 performs 
counting in opposite direction.
•
When the counter of slave channel 1 reaches 
0000H, it waits for a next interrupt from the 
master channel. When an interrupt is detected:
-
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
•
When the counter of slave channels 2 to 7 
reaches 0001H:
-
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
-
PWM output signals of slave channels 2 to 7 
are set/reset.
TAUAnTTOUTm of slave channels 2 to 7 outputs a 
PWM signal, a high-level signal or low-level signal 
depending on the values of real-time output bits 
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) and modulation output 
bit (TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm) of a pair of slave 
channels.
Stop Operation
Set TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously.
TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm is a trigger bit, which 
is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm is cleared to 0 and the 
counter stops.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnTTOUTm stop and 
retain their current values.

V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
13.14.11
Complementary Modulation Output Function
(1)
Overview
Summary
This function outputs a PWM signal, a high-level signal, or low-level signal 
from TAUAnTTOUTm depending on the real-time output bit value 
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) and the modulation output enable bit value 
(TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm) of a pair of slave channels. Three pairs o 
channels are typically used.
Prerequisites
• One master channel and seven slave channels
• The operating mode for the master channel should be set to interval timer 
mode. (See table 13-177, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Master Channels of 
Complementary Modulation Output Function.)
• The operating mode for slave channel 1 should be set to event count mode. 
(See table 13-181, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave Channel 1 of 
Complementary Modulation Output Function.)
• The operating mode for slave channels 2, 4 and 6 should be set to up/down 
count mode. (See table 13-184, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave 
Channels 2, 4, and 6 of Complementary Modulation Output Function.) 
• The operating mode for slave channels 3, 5 and 7 should be set to one-
count mode. (See table 13-188, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave 
Channels 3, 5, and 7 of Complementary Modulation Output Function.) 
• The output mode for master channels should be set to independent channel 
output mode 1. (See section 13.8, Channel Output Modes.) 
• This function does not use TAUAnTTOUTm of slave channel 1 but 
TAUAnTRC.TAUAnTRCm should be set to 1. (See section 13.8, Channel 
Output Modes.)
• The channel output mode for slave channels 2 to 7 should be set to 
synchronous channel output mode 2 with non-complementary modulation 
output. (See section 13.8, Channel Output Modes.) 
Functional
description
• Master channels: 
The counter of master channel is enabled by setting the channel trigger bit 
(TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm) to 1. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 1, enabling 
count operation. The value of data register (TAUAnCDRm) of master 
channel is loaded into the counter (TAUAnCNTm) and the counter starts to 
count down from this value.
When the counter of master channel reaches 0000H, INTTAUAnIm occurs. 
This decrements the counter value of slave channel 1 by 1 and the counter 
of slave channel 2 starts to count in the opposite direction.
• Slave channel 1:
When the counter reaches 0000H, slave channel 1 waits for the next 
interrupt from the master channel. And the TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded 
into TAUAnCNTm (slave 1) and INTTAUAnIm is generated.
Slave channel 1 is set as a real-time output trigger channel 
(TAUAnTRC.TAUAnTRCm = 1). The value of real-time output bit 
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm) of each channel is applied to the channel that 
detects the occurrence of an interrupt on slave channel 1. The real-time 
output bit value can be changed in any timing by application software but a 
new value is not applied until an interrupt occurs on slave channel 1.


=== PDF PAGE 752 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 732 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
• Slave channel 2:
When the slave channel 2 counter reaches 0001H, the slave channel 3 
counter starts counting down. When the slave channel 3 counter reaches 
0000H, an interrupt occurs.
• Slave channels 2 and 3:
The combined use of the master channel and slave channels 2 and 3 
generates a PWM output signal. The master channel generates a PWM 
output cycle, slave channel 2 generates a duty cycle, and slave channel 3 
generates dead time.
• Slave channels 4 to 7: 
Slave channels 4 and 6 operate like slave channel 2. Slave channels 5 and 
7 operate like slave channel 3.
A signal that is output from TAUAnTTOUTm depends on a real-time output bit 
value (TAUAnTRO.TROm), a modulation output bit value 
(TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm) an output level bit value 
(TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm) of the slave channel, as described in table 13-150, 
TAUAnTTOUTm Output from One Pair of Slave Channels of Non-
Complementary Modulation Output Function Type 1.
It is, however, prohibited that a high-level signal is output from both channel 2 
and channel 3 (in order to prevent a motor driver short circuit). It is necessary 
to postpone a transition to a high level by appending a delay to the channel to 
be switched to a high level in order to prevent both channels from setting high 
at the moment one channel is switched to a low level and the other to a high 
level. To achieve this, dead time set by a channel dead time output level 
register (TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm) value is appended to a normal or reverse 
phase PWM signal. 
The counter can be stopped by setting the TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master 
and slave channels to 1. This sets TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm to 0. TAUAnCNTm 
and TAUAnTTOUTm of master and slave channels stop but retain their values. 
Setting TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm to 1 restarts counting.
Conditions
• If TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm of a pair of channels is set to 1: 
– If TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of one channel is set to 1, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs the corresponding PWM of the channel.
– If TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of one channel is set to 0, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs a low-level signal.
• If TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm of a pair of channels is set to 0:
– If TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of one channel is set to 1, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs a high-level signal.
– If TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of one channel is set to 0, TAUAnTTOUTm 
outputs a low-level signal.
• If TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm is set to 1, high-level and low-level signals 
output from TAUAnTTOUTm are inverted. No PWM signals are changed 
depending on the setting of TAUAnTOL.TAUAnTOLm.


=== PDF PAGE 753 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 733 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
Note 1.
~PWM indicates an inverted PWM signal. PWM and ~PWM are set by 
TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm.
Note 2.
Any settings not listed above are prohibited.
• If TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm is continuously set to 1 while 
TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of one of paired channels is set to 1, full 
modulation is applied.
• If TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm is set to 1 at the first half of the period while 
TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of one of paired channels is set to 1, first-half 
modulation is applied.
• If TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm is set to 1 at the second half of the period while 
TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm of one of paired channels is set to 1, second-half 
modulation is applied.
• Whether dead time is added to a normal or reverse phase PWM signal 
when two channels become high-level signal outputs simultaneously 
depends on a TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm bit value.
– If TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm = 0, dead time is added to a normal phase 
PWM signal.
– If TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm = 1, dead time is added to a reverse phase 
PWM signal.
– The operation defined by a TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm bit value should be 
conducted by application software during operation. To modify 
TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm, rewrite it during the period when 
TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm is 00B.

1: INTTAUAnIm is generated and TAUAnTTOUTm is toggled 
at the beginning of operation or at a restart time.

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
Start Operation
Set TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously. (Set only slave 
channels 2 to 7 at a channel restart time.) 
TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm is a trigger bit, which 
is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm of master and slave channels 
is set to 1 and the counter starts to count down.
During Operation
TAUAnCDRm, TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm, 
TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm, and 
TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm can be changed at any 
time.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnRSF.TAUAnRSFm can 
be read at any time.
TAUAnRDT.TAUAnRDTm can be changed 
during operation.
TAUAnCDRm value of master channel and slave 
channels 2 to 7 is loaded into TAUAnCNTm perform 
count down. TAUAnCDRm value of slave channel 1 
is loaded and the counter waits for a master channel 
interrupt. When the counter of master channel 
reaches 0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm (master) is generated.
•
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into 
TAUAnCNTm to continue counting down.
•
TAUAnCNTm value of slave channel 1 
decreases by 1 and the counter waits for the next 
master channel interrupt.
•
TAUAnCNTm of slave channels 2, 4, and 6 
performs counting in the reverse direction.
•
The counter of slave channel 1 waits for the next 
interrupt from the master channel when reaching 
0000H. When the interrupt is detected:
-
TAUAnCDRm value is reloaded into 
TAUAnCNTm and the counter waits for the 
next master channel interrupt.
-
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
-
TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm is changeable.
•
When the counter of slave channels 2, 4, and 6 
reaches 0001H:
-
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
-
PWM output of slave channel m is reset.
-
TAUAnCDRm value of slave channels 3, 5, 
and 7 is loaded into TAUAnCNTm to perform 
count down.
•
When the counter of slave channels 3, 5, and 7 
reaches 0001H:
-
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
TAUAnTTOUTm of slave channels 2 to 7 outputs a 
PWM signal, a high-level signal or low-level signal 
depending on the values of real-time output bit 
(TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm), modulation output bit 
(TAUAnTME.TAUAnTMEm), and output level bit 
(TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm) of a pair of slave 
channels.
Stop Operation
Set TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously.
TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm is a trigger bit, which 
is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm is cleared to 0 and the 
counter stops.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnTTOUTm stop and 
retain their current values.

The above timing diagram shows how full modulation, fist-half modulation and

A modulated PWM output signal and TAUAnTRO.TAUAnTROm bit value are

TAUAnTDL.TAUAnTDLm bit values are specified by software, but a new 
setting is applied only when an interrupt occurs on slave channel 1.

normal and reverse phases.

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
13.14.12
Interrupt Sampling Function
(1)
Overview
Summary
This function divides the number of interrupts of the master channel by a value 
specified using a slave channel.
The interrupt sampling function is a sub function of the following functions:
• PWM output function 
(Section 13.14.1, PWM Output Function)
• Triangle PWM output function 
(Section 13.14.6, Triangle PWM Output Function)
• Triangle PWM output function with dead time 
(Section 13.14.7, Triangle PWM Output Function with Dead Time)
Prerequisites
• Two channels
• The operating mode for master channel should be set to interval timer 
mode. (See table 13-193, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Master Channels of 
Interrupt Sampling Function.)
• The operating mode for slave channels should be set to event count mode. 
(See table 13-196, TAUAnCMORm Settings for Slave Channels of Interrupt 
Sampling Function.)
• TAUAnTTOUTm is not used for slave channels of this function.
Functional
description
The counter (both master and slave channels) is enabled by setting the 
channel trigger bit (TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm) to 1. This sets 
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm, enabling count operation. The current value of data 
register (TAUAnCDRm) of master and slave counters is loaded into the 
counter (TAUAnCNTm).
• Master channels: 
When the counter of master channel reaches 0000H, INTTAUAnIm is 
generated and TAUAnCDRm value is loaded in TAUAnCNTm.
• Slave channels:
Every time the master channel generates an INTTAUAnIm, the counter of 
the slave channel decrements by one. When the counter reaches 0000H, it 
awaits the next interrupt from the master channel. This causes 
TAUAnCNTm (slave) to load the value of TAUAnCDRm, and an 
INTTAUAnIm is generated.
This function cannot make a forced restart. The counter can be stopped by 
setting TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and slave channels to 1. This sets 
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 0. TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnTTOUTm of master and 
slave channels stop but retain their values. 
Conditions
This function can conduct simultaneous rewrite. See table 13.7, Simultaneous 
Rewrite.


=== PDF PAGE 769 ===
R01UH0098EJ0100 Rev.1.00
Page 749 of 2239
Jul 25, 2012
V850E2/Px4
Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
(2)
Equations
Interrupt division operator = TAUAnCDRm (slave channel)
– One INTTAUAnlm is generated for the INTTAUAnlm count of the master 
channel defined by TAUAnCDRm (slave channel) + 1.

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
The following settings apply to the general timing diagram.
Master channels:
• INTTAUAnIm is generated at the beginning of operation. 
(TAUAnCMORm.TAUAnMD0 = 1)
Figure 13-105
General Timing Diagram of Interrupt Sampling Function
A simultaneous rewrite trigger signal is generated when the master channel
starts to count and the corresponding slave channel is at the peak of a
triangular wave.

(c)
Channel Output Mode for Master Channels

settings.
Table 13-195

Bit Name
Setting
TAUAnRDE.TAUAnRDEm
1: Enables simultaneous rewrite.
TAUAnRDS.TAUAnRDSm
0: Monitors master channel for simultaneous rewrite 
triggers.
TAUAnRDM.TAUAnRDMm
0: A simultaneous rewrite trigger signal is generated 
when the master channel counter starts to count.
1: Simultaneous rewrite trigger signal is generated 
when master channel counter is started and the 
corresponding slave channel is at the peak of 
triangular wave.
TAUAnRDC.TAUAnRDCm
0: Channel is not monitored for INTTAUAnIm signals 
used as simultaneous rewrite triggers. If 
TAUAnRDS.TAUAnRDSm = 0, master channel is 
monitored for simultaneous rewrite triggers 
regardless of the value of this bit.

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
(6)
Operating Procedure for Interrupt Sampling Function
Table 13-199
Operating Procedure for Interrupt Sampling Function  
Operation
TAUAn Status
Initial Channel Setting
Master channels: Set TAUAnCMORm/
TAUAnCMURm register and the channel 
output mode as described in (4) Register 
Settings for Master Channels.
Slave channels: Set TAUAnCMORm/
TAUAnCMURm register and the channel
output mode as described in (5) Register 
Settings for Slave Channels.
Set the value of TAUAnCDRm register of every 
channel. 
Channel operation is stopped.
Start Operation
Set TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously. 
TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm is a trigger bit, 
which is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm of master and slave channels is 
set to 1 and the counters of master and slave channels 
start counting down.
INTTAUAnIm is generated on the master channel.
During Operation
TAUAnCDRm can be changed at any time.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnRSF.TAUAnRSFm 
can be read at any time.
TAUAnRDT.TAUAnRDTm can be changed 
during operation.
TAUAnCNTm of master channel loads TAUAnCDRm 
value and the counter starts to count down. When the 
counter of master channel reaches 0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm (master) is generated.
•
TAUAnCNTm (master) loads TAUAnCDRm value 
and continues count operation.
•
TAUAnCNTm of slave channels counts down each 
time INTTAUAnlm of master channel is detected. 
When TAUAnCNTm of slave channels reaches 0000H:
•
INTTAUAnIm (slave) is generated.
Stop Operation
Set TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm of master and slave 
channels to 1 simultaneously.
TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm is a trigger bit, 
which is automatically cleared to 0.
TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm is cleared to 0 and the counter 
stops.
TAUAnCNTm and TAUAnTTOUTm stop and 
retain their current values.
 Restart

(7)
Specific Timing Diagrams
(a)
Interrupt Count (Master) = Interrupt Count (Slave)
Figure 13-106
TAUAnCDRm (Slave) = 0000H
• If TAUAnCDRm = 0000H, TAUAnCDRm value of slave channels is loaded 
into TAUAnCNTm each time INTTAUAnlm of master channel is detected. In 
other words, TAUAnCNTm is always 0000H.
• Therefore, an interrupt occurs on the master channel and simultaneously an 
interrupt occurs on slave channels.

Details of TAUAn Prescaler Registers
(1)
TAUAnTPS - TAUAn Prescaler Clock Select Register
This register specifies clocks CK0, CK1, CK2, and CK3_PRE for all channels 
of the PCLK prescaler. CK3 is generated by dividing CK3_PRE by the factor 
specified in TAUAnBRS.
Access
Readable/writable in 16-bit units.

<TAUAn_base0> + 240H

FFFFH
This register is initialized by any reset source.

TAUAnBRS - TAUAn Prescaler Baud Rate Setting Register
This register specifies the division factor of prescaler clock CK3.
CK3 is generated by dividing CK3_PRE by the factor specified in this register
plus one. The PCLK prescaler for CK3_PRE is specified in
TAUAnTPS.TAUAnPRS3[3:0].
Access
Readable/writable in 8-bit units.

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
(2)
TAUAnCNTm - TAUAn Channel Counter Register m
This is a channel m counter register.
Access
Readable in 16-bit units.
Address
<TAUAn_base1> + 80H + m  4H
Initial value
0000H or FFFFH
An initial value depends on an operating mode. See table 13-205, 
TAUAnCNTm Read Values after Re-Enabling Counter. This register is 
initialized by any reset source.
A read value depends on a counter value, a changed operating mode, 
TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm or TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm bit value.
The initial read value of the counter depends on an operating mode and how 
the counter is stopped.
• Stop by a reset 
• Stop by a counter stop trigger (TAUAnTT.TAUAnTTm = 1)
The following table lists the initial counter read values after the counter is 
stopped (TAUAnTE.TAUAnTEm = 0) and re-enabled (TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm = 
1).
The table also contains the counter read value one count after the counter is
enabled (TAUAnTS.TAUAnTSm = 1) with the counter waiting for a start trigger.

The same setting should be made for both even and odd slave channels in pairs.
These bit settings are applied when:
•
TAUAnTOE.TAUAnTOEm, TAUAnTOM.TAUAnTOMm, 
TAUAnTOC.TAUAnTOCm = 1

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
13.15.7
TAUAn DMA Window Registers 
(1)
TAUAnDASi - TAUAn DMA Window Address Setting Register i (i = 0 to 7)
This register specifies a window register address for DMA. Eight TAUAnDASi 
registers control 16 TAUAnDWRm registers. In other words, each TAUAnDASi 
register controls two TAUAnDWRm register.
Access
Readable/writable in 16-bit units.
Address
<TAUAn_base0> + 270H + m  4H
Initial value
0000H
This register is initialized by any reset source.
odd = 01, 03, 05, 07, 09, 11, 13, 15
even = 00, 02, 04, 06, 08, 10, 12, 14
The following table shows the relationship between the TAUAnDASi control 
registers and the TAUAnDWRm registers.

Section 13　Timer Array Unit A (TAUA)
Set TAUAnDASm [9:8] and [1:0] to 0.
(2)
TAUAnDWRm - TAUAn DMA Window Register m
This is a register for DMA (m = 0 to 15). TAUAnDWRm mirrors an address 
specified by the corresponding TAUAnDASi (i = 0 to 7) register. (See the 
description on (1) TAUAnDASi - TAUAn DMA Window Address Setting 
Register i (i = 0 to 7)).
Access
Readable/writable in 16-bit units.
Address
<TAUAn_base1> + 100H + m  4H
Initial value
0000H
This register is initialized by any reset source.

Description of TAUAnDASm Register

TAUAn

00H to 3CH: Specifies CDR0 to CDR15 registers.

48H: Specifies TAUAnRSF register.
4CH: Specifies TAUAnTRO register.
50H: Specifies TAUAnTME register.
54H: Specifies TAUAnTDL register.
58H: Specifies TAUAnTO register.
5CH: Specifies TAUAnTOE register.
60H to 7CH: Setting prohibited
80H to BCH: Specifies CNT0 to CNT15 registers.
C0H to FCH: Setting prohibited
